Not only does the price determine the market, especially when it comes to the health of food, but quality must also play a role. And our products have quality – regardless of whether organic or conventional production. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Production conditions, competitive opportunities and purchasing behaviour decide on the future of our small-scale agricultural agriculture. Only the correct interaction and mutual trust between politics, agriculture and consumers ensure the high quality of our food for the future.

However, I should like to point out briefly that this is really a harmless formal correction in the text, insofar as the tasks and functions of the forest are clearly and clearly regulated in § 6, namely in the form of the utility, the protection effect, the welfare effect and the recovery effect, where the protection effect is divided into the conservation forest and the object protection forest, and in the field of the welfare effect on the environment, in particular the balance of the climate and the water budget, and the cleaning and renewal of air and water. Both, the protection of forests and the welfare of forests, are defined in the form of a banana set-up by specific provisions with a view to achieving the objectives. But, ladies and gentlemen, it is not always the formal correctness. Of course, it is important that a law is formally correct, but especially important is the practice. It is particularly important that, in order to be able to fulfil the demands of the public in terms of the tasks and effects mentioned above, the professionally well-trained staff are available on-site. A lot of work is still ahead of us in this area, but this would ensure that there will be more food safety in the future. The common will to produce genetic engineering is important. This would ensure, as Mr Reheis has already said, that the market is supplied throughout Austria with food that is really the best. I would now like to make a total amendment. Total amendment Mr Grillitsch, Mr Wittauer, Mr Gradwohl, Mr Dipl.-Ing. Dr. Pirklhuber, colleagues "The competent members of the Federal Government are invited to: continue to take action at EU level against the re-admission of GMOs,’ – This is a very important point! –, ‘Incentive to the voluntary integration of non-genotechnic regions at EU level, in cooperation with neighbouring countries and in the national, regional and local areas, in particular taking into account the requirements for ecologically sensitive areas, organic farming and beekeeping and taking into account international agreements on biodiversity and biosphere protection’; – this ensures that genuine efforts are made to cooperate internationally and with the other countries in order to achieve these objectives together – "to use at EU level the possibility of creating non-genotechnic regions" – that is, I believe, a special mission to this government – ‘on the European level, continue to promote a Community-wide harmonised system of co-existence and liability, as possible contaminations with GMOs do not stop at the borders,’ – This concerns us the most; Co-existence should be defined at European level; ‘until the EU-wide liability regime implements a transparent liability regime under national legislation, including taking into account non-indebted and non-eligible impurities with GMOs, ensuring the simple enforceability of the provisions in neighbouring countries,’ – this also ensures that there is no risk that the farmer or producer alone bears this liability, but that one really sees that the polluter principle remains true – “support the Austrian plant breeders and the Austrian seed industry in its willingness to market GMO-free seeds and high-quality varieties,” – there is also a market; this may create an attractive market for some, because GMO-free also means that one must have GMO-free seed, and if one plays a pioneering role there, that is a good sign – ‘ensure transparent and clear implementation measures for the labelling of food containing genetic engineering in accordance with the applicable EU regulations in order to ensure the freedom of choice of consumers; and at European level, as low as possible as possible as possible at the technical limit for GMO seed contamination.’ I believe that this proposal will bring about something positive. Of course, we are supporting any far-reaching opportunity for citizens to implement these measures better, faster and in the interests of the citizens. That is why I am glad and happy that we have not only hoped but also worked together to make this request. – Thank you

ParlaMint-AT_2004-06-17-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00067_d7e9235	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Federal Minister! Mr Grillitsch rightly said at the beginning of this debate: It is a common effort in a sometimes different but very important question, such as genetic engineering, which repeatedly moves the entire Austrian society, from the consumers to the farmers. [[the speaker places a shopping bag with the inscription “Genfood? – No thanks!”]] These are initiatives from the natural food industry. I mention this without wanting to advertise individual companies here, but it is very interesting that the trading companies take this issue very seriously. We in politics too are required to respond to the demands, wishes and interests of consumers and to ensure food safety. That is why I think that this joint four-party request, Mr Federal Minister, should strengthen your back – and the colleague has rightly noted this here – because this is necessary, since the genetic engineering debate in Europe has not yet been completed. I also agree that there will be many questions, for example in individual areas of medical application, where we will see that it clearly brings more benefits than risks, where the benefits are overstated. But in the field of agro-geneic technology, Mr. Federal Minister, the application has so far been a real curse, even economically a curse, which is now confirmed by an imprecise body, namely the American Department of Agriculture (USDA). This U.S. authorities have recently found that the use of agrogen technology has so far ceased to produce in the US – on the contrary, conventional varieties at Soya have higher yields – and, on the other hand, the use of pesticides has not been reduced. On the contrary, the use of pesticides in the U.S. is increasing again because there is a phenomenon that we know from practice – that is nothing new in agricultural technology and agricultural science – that the uncrowds become resistant to the sprays. If only one means is used – in the case of Roundup – then very many uncrowds become very quickly resistant, and then there is a real agricultural problem again. But why do we also present our own motion for a resolution today? – Because we want to continue the discussion with you here in the House, in the committees, but also with the Minister, because we believe that this is very, very important to support our regions, our federal states. I myself was at the meeting of the non-genotechnic regions of Europe in Upper Austria, which took place at the initiative of the Greens and the Head of State. At this meeting there were twelve regions of Europe – and there are no small ones, such as Tuscany, but also areas such as Wales, Schleswig-Holstein – in whose regional administrations and political bodies there is a political will to take action here. In this regard, we shall put forward the following further motion for a resolution: The competent members of the Federal Government are asked to take the following measures: firstly, to create a transparent legal regime of liability in accordance with the 'polluter pays' principle – which is very important to us – within the framework of national legislation. – The 'polluter pays' principle is a principle which was also enshrined in the Genetic Engineering Act in Germany, and we believe that it would also be right in Austria to implement it in this way. In the Austrian Eco-Agricultural Programme (ÖPUL), it would also be a measure which is clearly EU-compliant, because the OPUL is a voluntary measure that would ensure that we are able to achieve GMO-free in the long term, regardless of which regulations are adopted in Europe. Thirdly, and I believe that this is the crucial directive that you, Mr Federal Minister, should initiate: a comprehensive framework law, a so-called genetic engineering precautionary law, on the establishment of non-technological farming areas in Austria, in particular with reference to protection requirements for ecologically sensitive areas, organic farming and beekeeping, and taking into account international agreements on biodiversity and biosphere protection. – It is therefore a question of supporting countries through a framework directive, a framework legislation to ensure that uniform standards are implemented throughout Austria. I would ask you to conduct this discussion process together with us, in the interests of Austrian agriculture and a non-genotechnic food industry in Austria. - Thank you.

Dr. Gabriela Moser: Life-long is more like...!]] A twelve-year term of office for the President of the Court of Auditors has been introduced in 1986, and I believe that this was an important and correct step at the time, because there has been the possibility of being appointed twice for six years, i.e. with the possibility of re-election. If you assume that – and this is also what is very much in the heart of the liberals when it comes to the President-in-Office of the Court of Auditors – it is an institution that is independent, which has been able to preserve its independence, then the current system is certainly a very good one. Because of the fact that there is no re-election, it is not dependent on future majority voting that you have to be re-elected if you want to, so that you may have to secure yourself by “weighted behavior”. – That’s how real independence has been given! With a twelve-year term of office, it is also possible, as I think, to move and produce a lot. The Court of Auditors, in general, represents a very, very important instrument for us parliamentarians, a control instrument with which we can look very much into the entire activity of a government. So this is a very important function for us. However, it is also important to accept the results presented by the Court of Auditors and to find them in order – and then not to discuss them again and again. But that's exactly what we're unfortunately experiencing: Despite the results of the Court of Auditors, and questions answered by the Court of Auditors, requests for the setting up of committee of inquiry have been made again and again. So once again there are questions that have already been clearly clarified by the Court of Auditors reports. The same is true of the request made by the Court of Auditors with regard to interception hunters, which has clearly been positive reports from the Court of Auditors on their ordering process. However, you from SPÖ are again asking again the same questions, which have been answered more than once by the Court of Auditors, in the form of an application for the setting up of an investigation committee, although, as has already been said, all of this has already been answered. The Court of Auditors considers this to be important – and this is what it has also written in its guide – and something that we all consider very important: increasing the efficiency of control in the public sector. This is a point that is important to all of us. However, the Court of Auditors also stresses its self-understanding, as taxpayers' attorneys against debt, against waste. That is also a very, very important task of the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors itself also sees itself as a model: with its employees and in its entire function, and it has therefore entered into its mission: active public relations, which, as I hear again and again, is something new and what President of the Court of Auditors has done in his function – and with his person – well. It is strengthened in its function as a consultative body, i.e. autonomously, and thus even more independent of the Federal Government and in the future also, in some way indirectly, the Parliament can advise. I welcome the improvements that have taken place during the Committee's negotiations. We were able to achieve important improvements in committee statements, such as the need to pay attention to taking into account gender parity, i.e. gender relationship, where it is legally possible, in order to establish and promote more women in science and research. Furthermore, the research strategy recommended by the Council of the Federal Government is to be presented, discussed and thus made public in Parliament. It will also be invited to the broad parliamentary opinion. Because this is not yet a legal basis, we can answer it in some way with a resolution. In addition, in the direction of Mr Broukal, the aim was to ensure multi-annual financing security, also with the help of a first committee statement to ensure that the multiannual planning is accompanied by a corresponding multiannual budgeting. I would like to have some time for the end of the speech to discuss with you about the SPÖ during the urgent debate and invite you to come to a four-party majority. I'm confident I see good signals. Let us take note of this parliamentary experiment! Thank you.

Finland – and with this one can perhaps scratch the curve to the remarks of Collaborator Fuhrmann – had up to the late 1970s a system of education which was almost identical to the Austrian one. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the SPÖ.]] Finland and Sweden have also carried out a very intensive educational discussion and have tried to make a general social consensus change in the education system. Among other things, reflecting the fact that developments in the Scandinavian countries, such as the increase in the female employment rate, have already existed much earlier – the employment rates of women are quite different there than in Austria – it becomes clear that certain consequences such as full-day school forms are a logical illustration of social conditions. It has tried to shape the school systems in such a way that social justice works half-wise and disadvantages are offset. How can improvements be made? Within 20 years, Finland has succeeded in pushing from a middle position in all studies – which can be verified – to the absolute top. And once you look in more detail and realize that – and this is the fascinating bad thing – the students in Finland bring the best performance and that the differences between the best students and those who bring the worst performance are only a fraction of the difference in Austria, you can't simply say that the organization of our school system is great. The Finns have a total school, so it's time, they have a full day school. They have a very large internal differentiation, which, by the way, is no contradiction at all, and, of course, they also integrate gift promotion. That's something you could look at more closely. Instead, you go here and say – I mean, it doesn't concern us, because you could write on many points about the fact that the Greens didn't say that anyway –: The SPÖ comes from the Stone Age! On the other hand, if you look at the Scandinavian countries, you can see that they have done exactly the success models that would be appropriate for Austria. I find this to be very embarrassing, how this debate is constantly being initiated by you! [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the SPÖ.]] Another point that is important to me and which I think is very important for the future of the school organisation: youth unemployment in Austria. The Austrian dual education model has led us to have a relatively low youth unemployment for years. That's right, my colleague, Fuhrmann, is right. However, if you look at the numbers more comprehensively, how it goes, then if you look at the unemployment statistics of the adults and see what training adults who are in long-term unemployment have, from where they come from in the school system, then you find on the other hand that the vast majority are coming from dual education. So they come from a training rail, which is designed to train manual skills. I am now slowly asking myself whether this dual education system, as we have – i.e. 15 out of the school system – is only a relatively short period of school education – whether this can be the answer of the future if you look at technological development in the teaching professions. And I'll tell you: It is no wonder that exactly the students are also highly affected by unemployment. – If you look at Finland on the other hand, you will notice two developments: Youth unemployment in Finland is falling, however, from a much higher level. Yes, Werner Amon! But let's look at the starting positions, otherwise these are only absolute comparisons that make little sense. A company like Nokia in Finland could be difficult to exist with a school system like we have. The development of research funding structures clearly takes account of European development. The recent publication of Statistics Austria on the occasion of the reform dialogue in May of this year – already mentioned – shows that the Austrian research rate for 2004 is expected to amount to 2.27 percent of GDP, the gross domestic product, i.e. that research and development expenditure will increase to a total of € 5.3 billion, despite relatively low economic growth. A remarkable brand! Based on these first results of the 2004 full survey, Statistics Austria strongly revised its estimates of research expenditure in April. This is gratifying to the extent that the already smiled goal of reaching a research rate of 2.5 percent by 2006 and to reach a research rate of 3.0 percent by 2010, is also really close and is not only vision or utopia. This shows the efforts of the government to do more for research and development than ever, especially by taking more money into their hands than ever before and also by stimulating the economy to make its contribution. I do not want to miss out on this, especially as it is currently being discussed why the Supervisory Board is so economically-friendly. Ladies and gentlemen! It is gratifying that Austria's research rate for the first time is significantly above the EU average. – This is also mentioned in particular. It should also be noted that over the past seven years, research expenditure in Austria has grown significantly more than twice as fast as the gross domestic product, as opposed to countries such as Germany or the USA, with a continuing trend. I would like to mention the joint institutions “National Foundation”, which secures €125 million per year of additional research money, and of course also the “House of Research”. Overall, these two priorities are the biggest reform of the Austrian research funding landscape for almost 40 years; exactly – we have found it in the Science Committee – it is 37 years. In 1967, the FFF and FWF funds were well known to be established, and so far this is the only real reform that will continue to lead to the future and make Austria's research landscape stand well internationally. Ladies and gentlemen! The law will enable us to set up this research promotion society in autumn, so that we will have a central institution in the future to promote economic research. In 2004, it will have a budget of almost €300 million, which will be increased to € 350 million by 2006. In this way, this Federal Government is making a clear commitment to the need to achieve the Barcelona objectives, which, as mentioned above, provide for a research rate of 3.0 percent of GDP. The new society will have clear and slim structures. It will bring together ASA companies, i.e. Austrian Space Agency, the Office for International Research and Technology Cooperation, the Research Fund for the Industrial Economy, FFF, and the Technology Impulse Society, the TiG. In concrete terms, this means that in future, instead of 15 bodies, only more than four are needed, and that is also a significant saving. It will be considerably simplified, significantly simplified, because there is only one point of contact and, of course, synergies that can be used to the best possible extent. The society will also – and this was also an old desire to create a demand – create multi-annual plans, which will finally give a planning certainty. I am also aware that the operation of this new society will only be ensured if the ministries work with the economy even better than before. The economy plays a very important role, so we are an important partner. I would draw attention to the fact that international benchmarking is common and that two thirds of expenditure from industry and industry and one third from public expenditure is known to be an ideal ratio. We are already 63 percent from industry and industry, as we have not been under 60 percent for too long. I would also like to stress this because sometime the question was raised here, which is why the economy in the Supervisory Board is so well represented. I think she's right. Ladies and gentlemen! A fear that has been expressed over and over again was that politics wants to interfere in the award of funding projects. In order to address this concern, we have explicitly noted that the technical decisions in the bottom-up field, whether a project is approved or not, will continue to be taken by an advisory board. This means, in summary, that: The proven core competences, structures and processes of all companies are preserved or improved from the point of view of customers, i.e. primarily in the interests of the researching companies, and secondly, the structural conditions are created to be able to maintain an active international presence and to be able to take real advantage of new opportunities. It is with this, I believe, also clear that all areas of the new society have their know-how to combine. Therefore, I can deliberately quote the philosopher of Austrian origin Sir Karl Raimund Popper, who once found very striking: “Our attitude towards the future must be: We are now responsible for what is happening in the future.” That is, as I mean, crucial and we have succeeded very well.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-06-17-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00067_d7e19007	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Mr. Vice-Chancellor! High house! Last but not least a brief overview of the debate and the law to be decided. The three most important measures were therefore the establishment of a company of its own, i.e. the merger of ASA, FFF, BIT and TIG – I think I no longer have to carry out these terms further, they have often been called enough – the Austrian Research Funding Society, a single contact person. The use of synergies et cetera, the expansion of RTD support and the lurching of EU funds were important. The second point is the new reformed science fund. This includes a Supervisory Board and multi-annual planning. I believe that this is also very important when looking to the future. The same applies to the Research and Technology Development Council. All of this, ladies and gentlemen, is based on a secure funding. There is a massive increase in funds of up to 50 percent compared to 2003 for the research promotion company, and there are also around €1.2 billion for the R & D sector as a whole. In total, we already have 185 000 employees in R & D, and we are on the verge of strengthening this number of employees with these measures.
